 suggesting that the model's performance might benefit from the more diverse    
 controlled generation process during training.                                 
 3. Inference time: Increased notably compared to previous runs. This is likely 
 due to the additional computations required for the expanded set of component  
 weights and the collection of more detailed statistics.                        
 4. KL divergence: Showed mixed results compared to Run 3. It decreased for the 
 circle and moons datasets but increased slightly for the dino and line dataset 
 This suggests that the impact of expanded component weights on sample quality  
 varies depending on the dataset.                                               
                                                                                
 The expanded component weights and detailed statistics provide valuable insigh 
 into the controlled generation process:                                        
                                                                                
 1. We can now analyze how different weight combinations affect the mean and    
 standard deviation of the generated samples for each dataset.                  
 2. The increased inference time indicates a trade-off between the level of     
 control/analysis and computational efficiency.                                 
 3. The mixed results in KL divergence suggest that the relationship between    
 component weights and sample quality is complex and dataset-dependent.         
                                                                                
 Next steps:                                                                    
 1. Analyze the collected statistics to identify patterns in how different      
 component weights affect sample characteristics across datasets.               
 2. Visualize the generated samples for each weight combination to gain insight 
 into the visual impact of controlled generation.                               
 3. Investigate whether certain weight combinations consistently produce better 
 results across datasets.                                                       
 4. Consider implementing adaptive weight selection based on the dataset        
 characteristics to optimize sample quality.                                    
 5. Explore techniques to reduce inference time while maintaining the benefits  
 detailed analysis and controlled generation.                                   
                                                                                
 ## Plot Descriptions                                                           
                                                                                
 1. Figure: train_loss.png                                                      
 Description: This figure shows the training loss curves for each dataset       
 (circle, dino, line, and moons) across all runs. The plot is organized as a 2x 
 grid, with each subplot representing one dataset. Each line in a subplot       
 corresponds to a different run, allowing for easy comparison of training       
 convergence across different experimental conditions. The x-axis represents    
 training steps, while the y-axis shows the loss value. This visualization help 
 in understanding how different modifications (PCA-alignment, controlled        
 generation, cosine beta schedule, and expanded component weights) affect the   
 training dynamics for each dataset.                                            
                                                                                
 Key insights:                                                                  
 - Comparison of convergence speeds across runs                                 
 - Identification of any unstable training behaviors                            
 - Assessment of final loss values achieved by different approaches             
                                                                                
 2. Figure: controlled_generated_samples.png                                    
 Description: This figure visualizes the controlled generated samples for each  
 dataset in the last run (Run 4: Expanded Component Weights and Detailed        
 Statistics). It is organized as a 2x2 grid, with each subplot corresponding to 
 one dataset. In each subplot, different colors or markers represent samples    
 generated with different component weight configurations. This visualization   
 provides insights into how manipulating the noise along principal component    
 directions affects the distribution of generated samples.                      
                                                                                
 Key insights:                                                                  
 - The spread and clustering of samples for different weight configurations     
 - How closely the generated samples match the original data distribution for   
 each dataset                                                                   
 - Identification of any outliers or unexpected patterns in the generated sampl 
                                                                                
 3. Figure: sample_statistics.png                                               
 Description: This figure presents detailed statistics of the generated samples 
 for each dataset in Run 4. It is organized as a 2x2 grid, with each subplot    
 representing one dataset. Each subplot shows multiple lines representing       
 different statistics (mean, standard deviation, entropy, and correlation) for  
 the first and second principal components across different weight              
 configurations. The x-axis represents the different weight configurations, whi 
 the y-axis shows the statistic values.                                         
                                                                                
 Key insights:                                                                  
 - How different weight configurations affect the statistical properties of     
 generated samples                                                              
 - Identification of trends or patterns in sample statistics across weight      
 configurations                                                                 
 - Comparison of statistical behaviors across different datasets                
                                                                                
 4. Figure: kl_divergence_comparison.png                                        
 Description: This bar plot compares the KL divergence values across all runs f 
 each dataset. The x-axis represents the datasets, while the y-axis shows the K 
 divergence values. Each group of bars corresponds to a dataset, with different 
 colored bars representing different runs. This visualization allows for easy   
 comparison of how different approaches affect the quality of generated samples 
 in terms of their similarity to the original data distribution.                
                                                                                
 Key insights:                                                                  
 - Identification of which runs produced the lowest (best) KL divergence for ea 
 dataset                                                                        
 - Comparison of the impact of different approaches on sample quality across    
 datasets                                                                       
 - Visualization of any consistent trends or unexpected results in KL divergenc 
 across runs                                                                    
                                                                                
 5. Figure: time_comparison.png                                                 
 Description: This figure consists of two bar plots comparing the training and  
 inference times across all runs for each dataset. The top plot shows training  
 times, while the bottom plot shows inference times. The x-axis represents the  
 datasets, and the y-axis shows the time in seconds. Each group of bars         
 corresponds to a dataset, with different colored bars representing different   
 runs. This visualization allows for easy comparison of computational efficienc 
 across different approaches and datasets.                                      
                                                                                
 Key insights:                                                                  
 - Identification of which runs were most efficient in terms of training and    
 inference times                                                                
 - Comparison of how different approaches affect computational requirements     
 across datasets                                                                
 - Visualization of any trade-offs between sample quality (from KL divergence)  
 and computational efficiency                                                   
                                                                                
 These visualizations collectively provide a comprehensive view of the experime 
 results, allowing for in-depth analysis of the effects of PCA-aligned diffusio
